公司簡介 
東陽成立於 1967 年，主營項目為汽、機車
零組件 ， 為台灣塑膠件供應商 ， 也是全球第
一大汽車 AM 塑膠件製造商，塑膠件全球
市佔率 70%、 板金部份市佔率38%。2Q26 
AM 營收佔比 75.68%，OEM 為 24.32%。 
AM 營收分布為北美 71%、中南美 11%、
歐洲 11%、中東 3%。OEM 市場為中國
64%、台灣 36%。

主要客戶：一汽 64%、特斯拉 11%、長安
汽車 10%、廣汽 4%

張予柔 yurou.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：電話訪談

東陽 (1319 TT) 
AM 產業長期正向

永豐觀點 
預估 2027 年 AM 營收增長 5~8%、OEM 年增 1~3%。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進建議 ：預估 2026、2027 年營收為 242.9、261 億元(-3.2%、+7.5%YoY)、
毛利率 32.7%、34%，營業利益為 40.5、49 億元(-12%、+21%YoY)，稅後淨利
為 36.1、40.2 億元(-4.9%、+11%YoY)，稅後 EPS 6.12、6.79 元。看好東陽最差
情況已過，即將迎來旺季，出貨將逐步重返正常增長軌道。目前評價交易在
13X2026 年 EPS(F)、11.9X2027 年 EPS(F)，為近五年本益比區間(10~18X)下緣，
本次以 2027 年 EPS 進行評價，給予 15X 本益比，目標價 102 元(15X2027 年(F) 
EPS 6.79 元)，維持買進建議。

ESG 評析 
東陽企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

公司簡介：東陽成立於 1967 年，主營項目為汽、機車零組件，為台灣塑膠件供應
商，也是全球第一大汽車 AM 塑膠件製造商，塑膠件全球市佔率 70%、板金部份
市佔率 38%。2Q26 AM 營收佔比 75.68%，OEM 為 24.32%。AM 營收分布為北
美 71%、中南美 11%、歐洲 11%、中東 3%。OEM 市場為中國 64%、台灣 36%。
1995 年起，公司陸續在中國重慶、南京、福州、長春、哈爾濱、敦陽、長春、天
津、武漢、長沙、廣州等地設廠，中國 OEM(非合併)主要客戶包含一汽 64%、特
斯拉 11%、長安汽車 10%、廣汽 4%。 
關稅底定，北美 AM 客戶回補庫存，3Q26 AM 營收年增 15.7%，重回成長：美
國 5 月下旬將台灣輸美汽車零件的 232 條款關稅調降至 15% 後，客戶結束觀
望，回補先前壓低的庫存，拉貨逐月增加，目前東陽工廠稼動率逾 90%，採兩班
制 24 小時生產。3Q26 AM 營收 45 億元(+5% QoQ、+15.7% YoY)，9 月 AM 營
收 14.96 億元(+0.85% MoM、+7.6% YoY)。 
AM 產業長期正面：北美維修量降幅收斂、副廠件占比創高；北美旺季即將到
來，客戶補庫存可期：北美碰撞維修量已從 2025 年的大幅衰退回到接近持平，
副廠件占比創新高，產業面已落底，結構變化對東陽長期有利： 
1. 汽車維修產業面：維修量衰退已收斂，且AM 件占比創新高：依美國理賠估價
平台 CCC Intelligent Solutions 統計(下簡稱 CCC)，2026 年 1~9 月全美理
賠件數降幅由去年同期的 8.3%縮小至 3.5%，可修復估價件數由 10.4%縮小
至 5.4%。北美大型汽車維修連鎖企業 Boyd 估計 2Q26 可修復理賠量持平至
年減 2%，與 CCC 方向一致。另，依 CCC 統計，2025 年副廠件占全美碰撞
換件金額 22.7%，為歷史新高；在維修量下滑的環境下，副廠件金額仍成長  
1.7%，原廠件則減少 1.8%。綜合上述，產業面已落底，有望回溫，而結構方
面，副廠件使用率持續提升，東陽為受惠者。 
2. 東陽客戶面：主要客戶 LKQ 於北美 AM 碰撞件優於市場：LKQ 2Q26 北美
零件及服務有機營收成長 0.5%，為九季來首度轉正，其中 AM 碰撞件營收
成長約 2%；同時，LKQ 公布其替代零件使用率(APU，即維修時選用副廠件、
而非原廠件的比例)連續兩季創下歷史新高，已突破 40%，背後原因是美國保
險公司近期理賠成本上升壓力較大，加上二手車價格走高，促使保險公司更傾

營運現況與分析 
圖一：東陽主要生產汽車塑膠及鈑金件

資料來源：東陽，Oct. 2026

### Page 3

3

向要求維修廠使用價格較低的副廠零件以控制成本 ， 顯示副廠零件滲透率提升
是長期趨勢，而非短期景氣波動。 
3. 研究部研判：AM 最差情況已過。維修量降幅收斂、副廠件占比提升，加上關
稅底定後的補庫存，再加上東陽每年投入平均約 18 億元開發新模具(今年為
25 億元)、持續擴大北美與歐洲保險公司認證，支撐 AM 營收回到成長軌道、
長期向好。惟 4Q25 受暴風雪帶動、基期較高，若今年冬天偏暖，則 4Q26
年增幅度可能有限；1Q27 則因 1Q26 低基期，年增表現較有利。 
短期負面因子：貨櫃仍偏緊，緩解速度取決於旺季強度：目前待出貨訂單累積 200
多櫃訂單，等待客戶安排貨櫃艙位，緩解速度需視旺季強度(採 FOB 報價，過往客
戶在需求好的時候願意加價取得貨櫃)。研究處認為目前客戶庫存約 1 個月，且旺
季(11 月~隔年 3 月)到來，預期有望逐步緩解。 
4Q26 毛利率有機會優於 3Q26：旺季稼動率有機會達 90%~95%(目前東陽工廠
稼動率逾 90%)，可攤提模具折舊等固定成本(AM 模具折舊約占 AM 成本 17%)。
旺季時北美銷售比重由淡季約 65%~66%回升至約 70%，有助產品組合。而市場
擔憂原物料上升的部分，截至九月來看，PP 較戰前上漲 5%，ABS 為 24%，但因
塑膠原料約 90%使用 PP、ABS 僅約 10%， 且東陽議價能力強 ， 成本上漲可轉嫁，
今年以來東陽產品售價已調整約 1.1%~1.5%，占比近 30%的自有產品轉嫁較快，
其餘產品約需一季~半年轉嫁，綜合以上，原物料上漲的部分對整體毛利率影響不
大。主要影響毛利率的因素在於產品組合(北美營收佔比、塑膠件比重)，以及稼動
率，故預期 4Q26、1Q27AM 毛利率將有望回升至 40%以上。 
預估 2027 年東陽 AM 營收重回正常成長軌道，年增 5~8%；OEM 年增 1~3%：
東陽是全球第一大汽車 AM 塑膠件製造商，塑膠件全球市佔率 70%、板金部份市
佔率 38%，其品項覆蓋範圍最廣，且模具持續增加，故在市況不明朗時，東陽仍
佔據產業領先地位及強議價能力，東陽2026 年預估將有 500~600 項 AM 新品通
過認證，新產品毛利率較高，搭配 Q4~Q1 迎來傳統旺季，預估 AM 營收有望持
續回溫。AM 市場的競爭重點便是模具數及規模經濟，參考東陽於 2024-2025 年
擴大模具開發套數及擴充產能，將有助於未來幾年 AM 營收的增長。而 OEM 方
面，台灣車市有望逐步復甦，且東陽客戶新案陸續放量，有望支撐2027 年營運表
現。中國 OEM 方面，今明年重點在於電動車轉型持續發酵，客戶展望正面，雖然
前幾年陸續關廠導致營收規模持續縮減， 但近期已調整告一段落， 預期整體產能利
用率能夠提升，推升毛利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估2027 年東陽 AM 營收重
回正常成長軌道，年增 5~8%；OEM 年增 1~3%。 
維持買進建議，長線投資價值顯現：預估 2026、2027 年營收為 242.9、261 億元
(-3.2%、+7.5%YoY)、毛利率 32.7%、34%，營業利益為 40.5、49 億元(-12%、
+21%YoY)，稅後淨利為 36.1、40.2 億元(-4.9%、+11%YoY)，稅後 EPS 6.12、
表一：北美維修量止跌、副廠件占比創高

## Document 025: 2026-10-22_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］みずほGDPナウ（2026年10月中旬時点）_f2dd2c14

### Page 1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 10 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.03％（年率＋0.10％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 10 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.10％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では10月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、8月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、9月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。

モデルによる10月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.03％ （年率＋0.10％）となった。 他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究所による10月15日時点の
ナウキャスティングが同＋0.29％（年率＋1.18％）、日本経済研究センターが公表した10月のESPフォ
ーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月30日～10月7日、回答者37名）における民間予測値平均が前期
比年率＋0.08％となっている。「みずほGDPナウ」の予測値は、民間コンセンサスよりやや高いもの
の、概ね近い伸びを予測していると言える。 
なお、多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしている理由の一つには、GDP統計作成上のテク
ニカルな要因がある。4～6月期には、 中東情勢悪化を背景に原油 ・ ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、
石油化学製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、
いずれもGDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％）
とプラス成長になったものの、 成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。
反対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の
下方圧力がかかる可能性がある。みずほ総合研究所が9月に公表したレポート2でも、こうしたテクニ
カル要因を考慮の上、7～9月期GDPは横ばい圏の伸びにとどまると予想している。 
■ 「みずほ GDP ナウ」による予測値下方修正の主因は生産・消費関連指標の悪化 
「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては10月中旬までに公表さ
れた月次経済指標を使って予測値を4回更新した。図表3では、予測値の更新過程と各月次経済指標の
寄与度を示している3。予測値が下方修正された主因は、8月の鉱工業生産、 消費財出荷指数の低下だ。
特に鉱工業生産指数については、7月末に発生した熊本地震による影響が下押し要因になったとみら
れる。 大手自動車メーカーで工場の操業が停止した影響で自動車が前月比▲6.8％と大幅に減産したほ
か、 電子部品 ・ デバイスも同▲1.9％の減産となった。7月調査時点の生産予測指数は前月比＋3.1％（ 補

### Page 3

3

正値ベース）と増産が見込まれていたが、大幅に下振れた格好だ。一方、世界的に旺盛なAI・データ
センター関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置を含む生産用機械などが増産したものの、熊本地震の影
響を打ち返せなかった。消費財出荷指数の低下については、4～5月の耐久財特需が一巡したことで反
動減の動きが生じた。非耐久財の出荷は前月から増加したものの、7月の落ち込みも踏まえると、 基調
としては力強さを欠いていると言えよう。長雨や低温、といった天候不順により生鮮野菜価格が上昇
しているほか、食肉も値上がりが大きく、こうした手に取る機会が多い品目の価格上昇が家計の節約
志向を強め、個人消費の下押し圧力となっているとみられる。9月についても、食品のほか、原油・石
油化学製品等のコスト高の影響で幅広い品目の価格上昇が加速する見込みであり、個人消費は冴えな
い動きが続く可能性がある。 
以上のように、7～9月期成長率は低い伸びに抑えられる公算が大きい。前述のとおり原油・ナフサ
の輸入増、 企業の在庫調整といった動きはGDP統計上、成長率に対してマイナスに寄与する。加えて、
熊本地震による生産減や天候要因による消費支出抑制の動きが下押し要因になる見込みだ。 
次回の 「みずほGDPナウ」については、11月16日に公表される7～9月期GDP1次速報を挟んで、10～
12月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組む。10月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて
推計し、12月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。原油高等を受けた物価上昇圧力の強まりや堅調
なAI・データセンター関連需要など、プラス・マイナス双方の影響が交錯する中、ナウキャストによ
る景気動向の把握はより重要なものとなるだろう。

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 詳細については、服部直樹「年率＋1.4％と 1 次速報から小幅に上方修正（4～6 月期2 次QE）」（2026 年9 月8 日）を参照され
たい。 
3 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。 隨著市場風險偏好下降 ， 避險資金轉向美國公債 ，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準 ， 法股單日下跌約1.5%， 歐元同步走弱 。 市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度 ，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇 ， 但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性 ，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變 。 隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升 ， 具技術優勢 、 財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利 ， 而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待 。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

## Document 125: 261027_daiwa_TPCA

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_TPCA.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show that 
was held on 20-22 October. Below, we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key 
company takeaways. Overall, we remain positive on Taiwan glass fibre, CCL, PCB, and 
substrate vendors, supported by upbeat company commentary. On pricing, we expect 
low-end CCL and E-glass price increases to be larger and more sustained than previously 
anticipated. High-end CCL vendor commentary suggests plans for more meaningful price 
increases from 2H26 to maintain their products’ relative profitability amid sharp increases 
in low-end CCL prices. We also expect prices for T-glass, ABF, CCL, and substrates to 
continue rising. On the CCL technology roadmap, we favour Q-glass combined with 
hydrocarbon resin (HC) over PTFE-based alternatives as the longer-term direction. Lastly, 
we provide our view on the CCL market competition landscape change after seeing 
Nan Ya Plastics targeting the high-end market more aggressively. We also provide some of 
our key highlights from related companies within the Taiwan PCB supply chain. EMC (2383 
TT, TWD4,750, Buy [1]), ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD746, Buy [1]), Kinsus (3189 TT, TWD941, 
Buy [1]) and NYPCB (8046 TT, TWD1,255, Buy [1]) are our top ideas within our coverage.

We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show last week. Below, 
we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key company takeaways.

Whether to adopt PTFE emerged as a key point of debate at the show.  
• During the TPCA show, Taiwan Union Technology (TUC; 6274 TT, TWD1,280, Buy [1]) 
unveiled its new M10 (Hydrocarbon base) and M11 (PTFE base) materials with glass-
fabric (Low-Dk2, Q-glass) and glass-fabric-free versions. Nan Ya Plastic (1303 TT, not 
rated) also unveiled its M11-grade material (ie, NPG-A99) at the show. Based on our 
understanding, NPG-A99 will continue to a hydrocarbon resin (HC) base, likely 
combined with Low-Dk2, to enhance CCL performance for high frequency and high-
speed signal transmission requirements. However, Elite Material (EMC; 2383 TT, 
TWD4,750, Buy [1]) remains reluctant to enter the PTFE-based CCL market, citing 
limited end application. Also, EMC plans to use advanced Q-glass for its M10 CCL. 
Lastly, we also heard from Panasonic’s booth representative that Panasonic (6752 JP, 
JPY3,687, Neutral [3]; covered by Ayaka Inomata) is pursuing a similar strategy for 
M9/M10 development, leveraging Q-glass instead of PTFE at this stage.

• While Nvidia (NVDA US, USD216, Outperform [2]; covered by Louis Miscioscia) 
appears keen to enhance product performance by adopting PTFE, given its very low 
dissipation factor (Df), PCB manufacturing yields remain a key concern. Accordingly, 
some leading vendors are broadening their material options (including PTFE-based 
CCL) to address potential customer requirements. EMC and Panasonic, however, 
remain firmly committed to a Q-glass-based technology roadmap. Although TUC 
highlighted progress on its PTFE-based M11 at a technology conference during the 
show, we believe it also intends to advance its existing HC resin/Low-Dk2 and HC 
resin/Q-glass roadmap for its next-generation CCL. In our view, its PTFE-based M11 
development is intended to preserve flexibility across potential technology pathways 
rather than replace its existing approach. Nan Ya Plastics’s strategy also appears 
reasonable: it can produce Low-Dk glass in-house but must source Q-glass and PTFE 
externally. This sourcing structure may explain its continued use of Low-Dk2 with HC-
resin instead of PTFE for its next-gen products, including M11.

 Taiwan PCB: Our key takeaways from 2026 TPCA show

### Page 2

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware

• Conclusion: which technology roadmap will prevail? We believe it is too early to 
identify a clear winner, as the supply chain continues to improve manufacturing yields 
for PTFE-based products. Some vendors remain cautious despite PTFE’s long history in 
the industry, as its thermal shrinkage and relative softness still make adoption in high-
layer-count (HLC) PCBs challenging. Given the current stance of leading global vendors 
EMC, Doosan (000150 KS), and Panasonic, together with EMC’s execution track 
record, we currently favour Q-glass-based solutions without PTFE as the more likely 
direction. Nevertheless, broadening product offerings to address different customer 
requirements should not be viewed negatively, provided vendors have sufficient 
resources to support development across multiple technology pathways.

Low-end CCL and e-glass continue to surprise on the upside.  
• During the show, we also spoke with Kingboard Laminates (1888 HK, not rated). The 
company remained positive on the E-glass outlook, although its booth representative 
provided no numerical guidance for next year following a c.200% price hike in 2026. 
Kingboard attributed the pricing strength to supply constraints, driven largely by AI 
server demand. While we highlighted increased glass yarn supply from Chinese peers in 
2Q/3Q26, the company argued that the weaving machine capacity remains the 
bottleneck. It expects to add only c.500 weaving machines in 2026 and 1.2k in 2027 (vs. 
current installed base of 3.3k). E-glass fabric price increases therefore appear 
supported by limited weaving capacity and producers shifting capacity from lower-end 
glass to higher-end products, including Low-Dk, Low-Dk2, and T-glass. Kingboard also 
noted a similar trend in low-end copper foil, where shortages have recently intensified.

• The read-through from Nan Ya Plastics is similar. The company indicated that its current 
fibreglass allocation between internal consumption and external sales is 45:55, with the 
internal share set to rise gradually as it upgrades its product mix. Its CCL portfolio 
remains concentrated in FR-4 (M2 grade), but it plans to migrate toward M4/M6, 
supported by long-term contract wins with two major hyperscalers seeking to secure 
CCL supply. In 2027, M4/M6 products are expected to account for 60-70% of its mix (4m 
out of 6.5m in capacity), with M2 and below contributing 20%. The remainder would 
comprise high-end CCL, including M7/M8/M9, reflecting recent development progress. 
We therefore expect Nan Ya’s external thin-type E-glass supply to decline as its M4/M6 
mix increases next year.

Competition from Nan Ya Plastics.   
• The current CCL shortage creates favourable conditions for Nan Ya Plastics to expand 
into the high-end CCL supply chain. Whether the company has entered AMZN’s T3/T4 
supply chain is also a key point of market debate. Based on discussions with its booth 
representatives, however, we believe management will prioritise upgrading its M4/M6 
product portfolio before pursuing the highest-end CCL market more aggressively. On the 
positive side, favourable supply-demand dynamics have supported Nan Ya’s transition 
from a low-end supplier toward the mid-to-high-end segment, although high-end CCL 
should still contribute a relatively small share of revenue in 2027. For incumbent 
suppliers, competition is clearly intensifying, but we expect the near-term impact on 
high-end peers to remain limited.

• Our read: We expect the impact on EMC and TUC to remain relatively limited in 2027. 
Competitive pressure could become more meaningful over 2028-2030, although it is too 
early to assess its magnitude.

### Page 3

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware 
27 October 2026 
3

Other key takeaways: 
• Nitto Boseki (3110 JP, JPY2,942, Neutral [3]; covered by Noritsugu Hirakawa) identified 
Taiwan Glass (1802 TT, NR) and Grace Fabric Technology (603256 CH, CNY84.95, 
Outperform [2]; covered by Dennis Ip) as key competitors. Its plan to triple capacity by 
2028 remains largely on track. The company also intends to expand capacity further by 
2030, although the details have yet to be confirmed.

• Resonac (4004 JP, JPY12,915, Outperform [2]; covered by Hidemitsu Umebayashi) 
indicated that customers are requesting ABF substrate core thicknesses of 2,000-
3,000μm, versus its current maximum of 1,600μm, to accommodate larger package 
sizes. Potential technology pathways include adopting glass cores or ceramic 
substrates, or stacking additional core layers to enhance rigidity. Based on discussions 
with booth representatives, substrate makers may move toward multi-core structures, 
which could increase T-glass consumption. However, technical challenges in enabling 
signal transmission between core layers remain a potential barrier that would need to be 
addressed over the next one to two years.

• Mitsui Kinzoku (5706 JP, JPY2,054, Outperform [2]; covered by Shinichiro Ozaki) 
indicated that, based on its technology roadmap, M10-grade CCL combined with its 
HVLP5 copper foil should support 448Gbps SerDes transmission. In the near term, the 
company plans to shift capacity from lower-end to higher-end HVLP products, with a key 
piece of production equipment scheduled for installation in 2028/29. On pricing, its booth 
representative suggested that quarterly increases of more than 10% would be 
appropriate. Regarding copper foil shortages highlighted by other supply-chain 
participants, the representative indicated that mid-tier products, such as HVLP1 and 
HVLP2, could face the most severe supply constraints.

• Unimicron (3037 TT, TWD1,060, Buy [1]) appeared to highlight multi-core structures and 
the embedding of passive components within core layers as key technology trends in 
ABF substrates.

• Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT; 4958 TT, not rated) indicated that its substrate business 
has a plan to list on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 1H27 under the name Leading 
Interconnect Semiconductor Technology (LIST). At the show, the company focused 
primarily on thick HDI boards and highlighted two next-generation technology trends: 1) 
sequential lamination (M+N) to improve signal integrity and increase routing density 
within the same footprint; and 2) hybrid HDI/HLC structures, which add HDI layers to 
thick HLC boards (M+N), as discussed earlier.

公司拜訪快報

曜越(3540)：2027 年 Gaming PC 與 AI 工 
建議：評等買進

一、曜越為電競電腦零組件大廠，積極切入AI 高效能工
作站系統 
曜越科技股份有限公司成立於 1999 年 11 月，為電腦機
殼、散熱器(模組)及電源供應器製造商，以自有品牌
Thermaltake 銷售桌上型電腦散熱模組、機殼、電源供
應器，專攻 clone 遊戲玩家市場，近年來公司積極整合
機殼、散熱、電源與系統配置能力，從單一產品銷售延
伸至完整 Gaming PC 系統解決方案，滿足高階 
Gaming PC 的效能與穩定性需求。4Q26 公司將切入
AI 工作站(AI Workstation)新業務 ，拓展地端 AI 運算
市場。2026 年 1~9 月主要產品營收占比，Gaming PC 
35%、電源供應器 33%、機殼 14%、散熱 13%、其他
5%。

二、2Q26 本業疲弱，EPS 0.01 元 
曜越 2Q26 營收 10.16 億元，年減 20.5%，毛利率則從
前期 28.1%升至 30.7%，主要是因記憶體漲價，使毛利
率較佳的客製化裝機業務佔比提升， 2Q26 稅後淨利
96.8 萬元，EPS 0.01 元。 
三、曜越從 PC 零組件品牌轉型為系統整合品牌，並積
極切入 AI 工作站市場  
展望下半年 ，3Q26 營收年減 2.9%， 估計3Q26 稅後 EPS 
0.09 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上 AI 工作站從雲端到
地端的基本條件已於 2026 年第三季成熟，產品規劃完
成，第四季開始進入市場，出貨量將逐步上升。 
2026 年前九月 Gaming PC 營收佔比已達 35%， 超越電
源供應器的 33%，成為最大產品線，象徵系統整合業務
已成為營收核心。曜越的電競 PC 分為三個價格層次：
入門級 1,599-1,799 美元、進階級 2,599 美元、高階
4,399-4,499 美元。其中核心零組件如機殼、散熱、電源
皆是自製，而主機板、CPU、GPU、SSD 等為外購，透
過美國、德國、澳洲、中國等海外子公司進行在地組裝
與銷售。展望下一階段，曜越將複製電競 PC 的成功模
式，推出五款 AI 工作站與 AI PC 產品線，從入門級 AI
電競 PC（View 380 XL，1,801 TOPS）到企業旗艦雙
系統 AI 工作站（A1500 Dual，16,000 TOPS） ，搭載
NVIDIA RTX 5090、RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 及 AMD 
Radeon AI PRO R9700 等高階 GPU。AI 工作站從雲端
到地端的基本條件已於 2026 年第三季成熟，產品規劃
完成，第四季開始進入市場，2027 年營收貢獻將逐季顯
現 ， 目標2027 年占整體業績一成 ， 主要鎖定AI 開發者、
專業創作者、學術科研及中小企業等應用。這代表曜越
正式從傳統電競跨足企業級 AI 運算、專業開發與數位
創作的地端商機，由於商用工作站毛利通常優於一般消
費級產品，這將是明年拉升獲利結構與擴展新市場的最
大看點。 
四、評等買進理由 
投顧認為曜越已從機箱、電源供應器及散熱等 PC 零組
件，轉型至拓展更高毛利率的電競PC 系統整合與 AI 工
作站業務，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 30%以上，
預期 2027 年營收將成長 15%，估計 2026 年 EPS 0.46
元，2027 年 EPS 1.95 元，給予買進的建議，目標價31
元。(16x2027EPS)。

研 究  報 告                
力成(6239.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
4Q26 營收維持高檔，關注高階封裝業務布局進度  
 投資評價與建議 
我們認為高階封裝業務進度如期，且為明年公司重點布局業務，整體狀況與原
先預期差異不大；我們給予公司18倍PE，為近期股價修正前之平均評價水準。 
 重點評論分析 
估4Q26營收維持高檔：當前公司封裝、測試產能利用率均達90%以上，推估
營收動能包含：(1)記憶體需求維持高檔，新業務TSV DDR5開始量產，另公司
持續因應記憶體報價上漲 ， 適時反映於封測報價 ； (2)邏輯業務中 ， FCBGA需求
維持穩健且公司將於本季導入更大尺寸封裝 ， 子公司超豐Flip Chip月產能於年
底擴充至2.3億顆，至於Tera Probe/Terapower除了有AI/MML需求支撐外，
⾞用需求回溫亦帶來成⻑動能。估4Q26營收季增5.8%至268億元，毛利率
24.1%，稅後EPS 3.8元。 
高階封裝業務逐步發酵、封測報價議價能力強勁支撐2027年成⻑動能 ：展望
2027年，基於：(1)DRAM市場供不應求至少延續至 2027年，機構估2027年
DRAM報價續漲但漲幅收斂，推估將持續支撐漲價成本之能力；(2)邏輯業務
中，封測產業產能利用率維持高檔，持續支撐公司議價能力，而公司高階封裝
比重成⻑帶動產品組合持續轉佳，如FOPLP明年有望開出3k月產能，子公司
Tera Probe/Terapower持續搶攻AI測試業務，另Terapower新竹新廠將於明
年貢獻營收。預估2027年營收年增22.5%至1,182.4億元，稅後EPS 18元。 
 投資風險 
FOPLP進展不如預期。

| 營運概況  
3Q26本業表現大致符合預期：力成3Q26營收季增9.6%至253.3億元，毛利率24%，稅後EPS 3.44元，其中本業表
現大致符合預期，營收動能來自於記憶體需求維持穩健，子公司超豐價格調漲和客⼾需求成⻑，T e r a  
Probe/Terapower持續受惠AI/MML、HPC封測訂單支撐，而獲利方面則有價格調漲、高產能利用率(力成3Q26封
裝、測試產能利用率分別為85~90%、85%)之挹注，帶動毛利率季增2.3ppts。 
估4Q26營收維持高檔：當前公司封裝、測試產能利用率均達90%以上，推估4Q26營收動能包含：(1)記憶體業務需
求維持高檔，其中企業級SSD持續受惠於AI伺服器需求，另TSV DDR5開始量產，估月營收貢獻約1~2億元，價格
方面，公司持續因應記憶體報價上漲並適時反映於封測報價；(2)邏輯業務中，FOPLP驗證進度如期推進，FCBGA
需求維持穩健且公司將於本季導入更大尺寸封裝(78mm*88mm)，子公司超豐Flip Chip月產能於年底擴充至2.3億
顆 ， 另將部分不具成本優勢之產品轉移至菲律賓廠量產 ， 至於Tera Probe/Terapower除了有AI/MML需求支撐外 ，
⾞用需求回溫亦帶來成⻑動能。我們估4Q26營收季增5.8%至268億元，毛利率24.1%，稅後EPS 3.8元。 
高階封裝業務逐步發酵、封測報價議價能力強勁支撐2027年成⻑動能：展望2027年，基於：(1)DRAM市場供不應
求至少延續至2027年，機構估2027年DRAM報價續漲但漲幅收斂，推估將持續支撐漲價成本之能力，至於NAND
市況據機構預估明年仍有個位數的缺口或趨於平衡，僅有企業級SSD需求仍維持強勁；(2)邏輯業務則為明年最大成
⻑動能，基於整體產業產能利用率維持高檔，支撐公司議價能力，加上高階封裝比重成⻑帶動產品組合持續轉佳，
包含FOPLP明年有望開出3k月產能，推估應用為AMD之電競晶片，子公司Tera Probe/Terapower除了既有業務持
續成⻑外，也積極搶攻美系客⼾下⼀代AI ASIC測試商機，另Terapower新竹新廠也將於明年開始貢獻營收(估月營
收貢獻約2~3億元)。預估2027年營收年增22.5%至1,182.4億元，毛利率24.5%，稅後EPS 18元。

5

玉山證券投資顧問股份有限公司（以下簡稱「玉山投顧」）為玉山金融事業群之成員。

研究報告可自本公司網站( www.esunconsulting.com.tw )取閱。

投資評等說明 
買進：未來六個月內潛在漲幅 30%以上。 
增加持股：未來六個月內潛在漲幅 15~30%(含)。 
中立：未來六個月內漲跌幅-15~15%(含)。 
降低持股：未來六個月內潛在跌幅 15%以上。 
未評等：無。

## Document 228: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율

## Document 251: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2 분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2 분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4 분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 254: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

2026-09-30

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

매수(유지)

Key: 하이닉스 자사주 소각으로 지분가치 증가 및 지분율 상승 효과 
Step: SK 하이닉스의 추가적인 주주환원도 주가 모멘텀

The Japan Research Institute, Limited

1 
Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

### Page 6

6 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

## Document 323: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-30)

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%
RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

## Document 330: FX Daily_ Can France wake up the ECB doves_

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Can France wake up the ECB doves_.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The ECB will likely keep rates on hold today, but we don’t think
French-related risk is enough to drive a material dovish shift
yet, especially with energy prices running so high. We expect a
contained euro impact. After a slightly negative reaction to
yesterday’s FOMC, we see the dollar ﬁnding some support from
today’s US PCE and GDP data

Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Chris Turner
We don't expect a dovish pivot from the ECB today
USD: Don't read too much into the
softish reaction to the Fed
The dollar traded a little softer after the Fed’s hold yesterday, with
the USD 2yr swap rate falling around 8bp. As discussed in our
review note, the overall message remained hawkish, with Chair
Warsh reiterating his ﬁrm commitment to price stability, adding
that current inﬂation “remains elevated” while the economy is
“expanding at a strong pace”. The modest dovish market reaction
was probably due to the lack of clearer guidance towards a

### Page 2

December hike, although we’ve now learned not to expect that from
Warsh.
On the vote split, Beth Hammack and Lisa Cook’s dissents in favour
of a hike were not particularly notable signals in themselves. Cook’s
vote is a bit of a surprise, however, given she was considered a more
centrist member, and may fuel further ire from the Trump
administration, which is still attempting to remove her.
From an FX perspective, the picture for the dollar doesn’t change

after this Fed meeting. A December hike remains in play (21bp
priced in) and its prospect should prove fairly resilient to a potential
oil price decline. We see limited room for bearish follow-through to
yesterday’s mild negative USD reaction on the Fed story alone. If
anything, some positioning for a softer USD could emerge ahead of
next week’s US midterms. Barring that, consolidation of the dollar’s
recent gains looks more likely.
Today, we expect September core PCE at 0.3% MoM, in line with

consensus and reinforcing the message of a hot inﬂation
environment. Third quarter growth should also be robust at 3.4%
QoQ annualised (consensus 3.2%), with consumer spending and
tech investment continuing to propel the US economy forward. We
expect a modest positive dollar reaction to today’s numbers.
Francesco Pesole
EUR: We don't expect a dovish pivot
The European Central Bank is likely to keep rates on hold today.
We’ve outlined four scenarios in our ECB cheat sheet: three involve a

hold, while one very hawkish scenario sees a hike. Our baseline
view is neutral. We don’t think we are at the stage where French
bond market turmoil can trigger a signiﬁcant dovish turn by the
ECB, especially with such elevated oil and gas prices (TTF hit
€100/MWh in Europe yesterday).
We expect much of the market reaction to be driven by the ECB’s
tone on France and the prospect of another hike this year. Markets
are pricing in 21bp for December, and in September oﬃcials

signalled that a rate hike by year-end was in play in their

### Page 3

customary tip to the media a few hours after the rate
announcement. Signalling that rates are “likely” to be raised in
December would constitute a modest hawkish surprise in our view.
However, we see a slightly less committal message as more likely,
perhaps that a December hike is “possible” or “in play”.
Either way, we don’t expect this meeting to be a turning point for
the euro. EUR/USD has shown it’s not yet ready to break below
1.110, and we don’t expect today’s ECB risk event to trigger a major

leg lower. A slightly stronger USD on US data may, however, keep
modest pressure on the pair.
Francesco Pesole
CAD: More tightening will come
The Bank of Canada raised rates by 25bp to 2.50% yesterday, in
line with market expectations. Stronger activity and growing
concerns about cost pass-through suggest this is unlikely to be a
one-and-done move, although policymakers stressed they will
proceed carefully. We expect another 25bp hike in 1Q27, most likely

in January, with the risk that persistently high energy prices bring
that move forward to December.
That said, we think markets are too hawkish in pricing around 90bp
of tightening over the next 12 months. The BoC’s central estimate
of neutral is around 2.75%, and while second-round inﬂation eﬀects
should emerge as ﬁrms pass higher costs to consumers, we expect
them to remain contained, particularly when it comes to wage
growth. Our base case therefore remains that rates peak at 2.75%.

For CAD, oil remains the key swing factor. High prices can keep
December hike expectations alive and support the currency, but we
remain bearish on oil and think a correction would trigger dovish
BoC repricing. We therefore see limited room for CAD gains from the
domestic story alone and retain our year-end USD/CAD target of
1.420.
Francesco Pesole

CEE: The Fed offers a chance to stabilise,
but gas is inviting further weakness
The CNB’s "blackout period" begins today in the Czech Republic,
and the market is returning following yesterday’s public holiday.

On Tuesday, another board member, Jakub Seidler, conﬁrmed his
intention to vote for a rate hike at next week’s meeting. Given that
he has consistently voted in line with the board majority, we believe
a 25bp rate hike – bringing the rate to 4.00% – is a done deal. In our
CNB preview published yesterday, we noted that the market is
primarily driven by energy prices, with the CNB having relatively
little impact. As the Czech market resumes trading after

yesterday’s holiday, we expect a catch-up with PLN rates, likely
resulting in an upward move of roughly 8bp.
Yesterday’s Fed meeting has only limited implications for the CEE
market. Some relief in the EUR/USD pair, combined with a return of
optimism to equity markets, oﬀers a chance for CEE currencies to
stabilise today. In the medium term, however, TTF gas prices
surpassed €100 yesterday, a factor fuelling further sell-oﬀs across
the CEE region – and the rationale behind our bearish outlook on

CEE currencies remains intact.
Frantisek Taborsky

Key takeaways
2ECONOMIC RESEARCH
1 A rise in inflation still confined to energy
Rising energy prices are pushing
inflation higher in Europe and the
United States, while energy’s
contribution is slightly negative in
Japan.
3 Mounting pressure on producer prices
2 Some recent pressure on core measures
4 Rising short-termmarket expectations
Markets (swaps) are pricing this
rise in inflation into short-term
measures. Long-term expectations
(5y5y) are rising in Japan and the

UK.
Over recent months (3m/3m), core
indices have been firming in the
euro area and Japan: a signal to
watch.
In Europe and Japan, pressure on
producer prices is building. The
risk of the energy shock spilling
over to other CPI items is
increasing.

From:    陸⾏之  Andrew Lu on global semis and techs <andrewhclu@substack.com>

存儲記憶體⼤漲價造成的  PC 需求不振，外加以⾊列廠爆炸停⼯影響
數據中⼼晶⽚⽣產，造成  Intel 四季度營收，⽑利率及 EPS 都不如市

ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS

READ IN APP
Great improvement in 3Q26

Intel reports 3Q26 sales of US$16.5bn, 2% qoq and 21% yoy, in line
with Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 gross margin of 43% was 2ppts
higher than Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 EPS of US$0.6 is much
higher than Bloomberg’s US$0.28 largely due to 2ppts better gross
margin, 4ppts better operating margin and non-operating income of
US$1.1bn.
Data center IC sales beat: 3Q26 Data center IC sales (largely
server CPU sales) were US$7.32bn, up 17% qoq and 78% yoy,
nearly 10% above consensus estimates. Data center IC sales

accounted for 44% of 3Q26 sales, up from 2Q26’s 39% and from
3Q25’s 30%. Operating margin for data center IC sales was 42%,
much higher than Bloomberg’s 27%, and improved from 2Q26’s
40% and 3Q25’s 23%.
Improve in progress for foundry business: In addition to
stronger data center IC sales, Intel foundry 3Q26 sales improved to
US$6.7bn, up 16% qoq and 58% yoy. Driven by higher utilization
rate, 3Q26 operating margin for Intel foundry was improved to -28%

### Page 2

from 2Q26’s -36% and 3Q25’s -55%, which was much better than
Bloomberg’s -36% estimates.
Source: Intel, Bloomberg est.

### Page 3

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
4Q26 guidance missing on sales, margin, and EPS
Combined eﬀects from lack of demand on client computing (due to
memory price hike and PC customers to aggressively manage
inventory might extend to 1H27) and lack of data center CPU
capacity (limited by in house capacity after Israel Kiryat Gat fab
factory shut down), Intel guides 4Q26 sales of US$15-16bn, down 3-
9% qoq and 9% below Bloomberg estimate of US$17bn (Fab shut

down aﬀects 2ppts sales). 4Q26 gross margin is guided to
deteriorate to 37% (Fab shut down aﬀects 2ppts margin), 6ppts
lower than 3Q26’s 43% and Bloomberg’s 42.8%, resulting much
lower diluted EPS of US$0.1 (Bloomberg’s US$0.3).
